And then when I finally do fall asleep, I usually wake up around 4 a.m., and I just never really get back to sleep after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Um, I guess I've been losing weight. I'm not really trying to, but my clothes are definitely fitting a lot looser than they did a year ago. It's just... most school days, I either completely forget to eat or just avoid it until late in the afternoon. I just don't have much of an appetite.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Um... I don't think I'm talking or moving any slower than normal. And I'm not really restless or fidgety, either. I just feel exhausted, but physically, I think I'm moving around the same as I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way, with little interest or pleasure in things and feelings of worthlessness, for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not quite two years. It's been going on for about a year and a half, maybe a year and seven months. It started right around the time I agreed to take over the STEM assessment redesign for the school, which was on top of my regular teaching load. That's when all of this really started to hit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before in your life, where you felt similarly, or is this the first time you've experienced these thoughts and feelings?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um... this is really the first time I've ever felt like this. I mean, I've had normal stress from teaching before, of course, but nothing like this. I've never experienced these kinds of feelings or thoughts until everything piled up a year and a half ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of anxiety or nervousness lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm overwhelmed by the workload, but I wouldn't call it anxiety or nervousness. It's more just... this heavy feeling of being drained all the time, not like I'm on edge or panicking about things. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much been a steady decline since it started. I wouldn't say there was a specific time where it was suddenly a lot worse... it's just been this constant, heavy feeling that hasn't really let up for the past year and a half.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific that happened around the time you took over the STEM assessment redesign that you think might have triggered these feelings?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... there wasn't any big life event or anything like that, if that's what you mean. It was really just the redesign itself. Taking that on just added so much to my plate on top of my regular teaching schedule. So, no, nothing else specific happened around that time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these thoughts and feelings have been interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life most of the time, or is it more that they're just really unpleasant but you're still managing to get by?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's definitely interfering most of the time. I wouldn't say I'm just managing to get by anymore. Um, I'm falling behind on my grading and lesson planning, and it's getting to the point where it's actually affecting my classroom. I even made a grading mistake recently that really scared me, because I used to be so meticulous about my work. \n\nAnd it's not just at school, either. I've basically stopped showing up for my friends and family the way I used to. It feels like my whole life has just narrowed down to barely getting through the school day, and then going home completely exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced two months or more where you didn't have these feelings of worthlessness and lack of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Since this all started about a year and a half ago, it's just been constant. There hasn't been a break at all, let alone a couple of months where I felt normal or interested in things again. It's just been this same heavy feeling the whole time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I'd say about a year and seven months. I used to really look forward to things like playing softball, going to my book club, working in my garden... even the robotics club I help with at school. But now, I mostly just go through the motions if I actually show up. A lot of the time I just end up canceling, honestly, because none of it really feels enjoyable to me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you're able to get things done?\n\nPatient: Yes, my energy has definitely been a lot lower. It's like... by the time I get through a day of teaching and then a department meeting, my body just feels incredibly heavy. Getting anything else done after that, even just making dinner or trying to answer a few emails from parents, it just feels like climbing a steep hill. I just don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's actually really frustrating because I can be completely exhausted, but I'll still just lie awake in bed. Um, I guess I've been losing weight. I'm not really trying to, but my clothes are definitely fitting a lot looser than they did a year ago. I just don't have much of an appetite.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that.
